Installation Mobile Phone App<\/a><\/li>\n<li><a href=\"#a/14059563735565\">Symbio™ 700 - Bluetooth Connection Instructions<\/a><\/li>\n<li><a href=\"#a/16561370756237\">Symbio™ 700 - Precedent or Odyssey - How to configure or edit configurations using the Mobile Phone App<\/a><\/li>\n<\/ul><h2>How many wires do I need? <\/h2><p>1 - Up to 7 conductor thermostat wires between the thermostat OR zone sensor and condenser<\/p><p>1 - Up to 6 conductor control wires between the AHU and condenser<\/p><p>2 - 2-conductor twisted, shielded, stranded communication wires between the AHU and condenser. <\/p><h2><span><strong>Cable Identification<\/strong><\/span><\/h2><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_32480481608205_mceclip0.png\"><\/p><h2>Field Wiring<\/h2><h3>\n<span><strong>AHU Wiring to Condenser<\/strong><\/span><br><span><strong>Cable #1 Modbus Communication<\/strong><\/span>\n<\/h3><p><strong>Cable #1 <\/strong>is a Modbus communication link between Symbio 700 and the AHU VFD. It is required for Pairing B as the AHU uses a communicating VFD. This cable must be a 2-conductor, twisted, shielded, stranded cable intended for 2 wire communications. We recommend Trane Purple Comm wire (Windy City Wire Spec #0523003) or any 2 conductor comm wire. 3 Conductor comm wire is also acceptable for Cable #1.  <a href=\"#a/360055739011\"><em>Symbio Communication Wire<\/em><\/a><\/p><div><p><strong>DO NOT USE THERMOSTAT, ETHERNET OR NON-COMMUINCATION RATED WIRE FOR COMMUNICATIONS<\/strong><\/p><\/div><p><span><strong><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_32480440579085_mceclip0.png\"><\/strong><\/span><\/p><p><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_32480440581005_blobid1.png\" alt=\"\"><\/span><\/p><h3>\n<span><strong>AHU Wiring to Condenser<\/strong><\/span><br><span><strong>Cable #2 Control Wiring<\/strong><\/span>\n<\/h3><p><span><strong><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055517711_26695658206989_mceclip0.png\"><\/strong><\/span><\/p><p><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055517711_26695658219789_blobid0.png\" alt=\"\"><\/span><\/p><h3>\n<span><strong>AHU Wiring to Condenser<\/strong><\/span><br><span><strong>Cable #3 IMC Communications<\/strong><\/span>\n<\/h3><p><strong>Cable #3 <\/strong>is an IMC communication link between Symbio 700 and the Indoor Option Module. It is required for electric heat operation and VVZT configuration. This cable must be a 2-conductor, twisted, shielded, stranded cable intended for 2 wire communications. We recommend Trane Purple Comm wire (Windy City Wire Spec #0523003) or any 2 conductor comm wire.  <a href=\"#a/360055739011\"><em>Symbio Communication Wire<\/em><\/a><\/p><div><p><strong>DO NOT USE THERMOSTAT, ETHERNET OR NON-COMMUINCATION-RATED WIRE FOR COMMUNICATIONS<\/strong><\/p><\/div><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055517711_33544754640653_image.png\" width=\"888\" height=\"151\"><\/figure><p><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_32480481617805_blobid1.png\" alt=\"\"><\/span><\/p><h3>\n<span><strong> Wiring Thermostat to Condenser<\/strong><\/span><br><span><strong>Cable #4A Thermostat Wiring<\/strong><\/span>\n<\/h3><p><strong>Cable #4A<\/strong> is for conventional thermostat use or when the condenser is controlled by relays. Unit will only use Cable #4A or 4B, not both. <\/p><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_33507515121933_image.png\" width=\"888\" height=\"268\"><\/figure><p><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_32480481620365_blobid2.png\" alt=\"\" width=\"227\" height=\"366\"><\/span><\/p><h3>\n<span><strong>Wiring Zone Sensor to Condenser<\/strong><\/span><br><span><strong>Cable #4B Zone Sensor Wiring<\/strong><\/span>\n<\/h3><p><strong>Cable #4B<\/strong> is for zone sensor use and is required when VVZT (SZVAV) operation is desired. Zone sensors are commonly used with BAS or can be standalone. Unit will only use Cable #4A or 4B, not both.<\/p><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_33507492841997_image.png\" width=\"890\" height=\"229\"><\/figure><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_32480440592653_mceclip4.png\"><\/p><p><span>*Zone sensor wiring shown for BAYSENS135, other mechanical zone sensors similar.<\/span> <\/p><h3>\n<span><strong>Emergency Stop Wiring<\/strong><\/span><br><span><strong>From Stop Devices to Symbio 700<\/strong><\/span>\n<\/h3><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_33507492847885_image.png\" width=\"888\" height=\"128\"><\/figure><p><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/32480440606861_46577899085837_image.png\" width=\"612\" height=\"464\"><\/span><\/p><p>Wiring above reflects normally closed safeties. Typically, condensate overflow switches (or pan switches) are normally closed devices. If using normally open devices, they can be wired in parallel, and the Customer Connection Polarity does not need to be changed.<\/p><p>For more information, see the links below:<\/p><ul>\n<li>Trane Commercial Help Center - <a href=\"#a/360055257251\">Symbio™ 700 - Emergency Stop Input<\/a>\n<\/li>\n<li>Trane Commercial Help Center - <a href=\"#a/360060801931\">Symbio 700 Demand Limit and Emergency Stop Polarity<\/a>\n<\/li>\n<\/ul><h2><strong>Configuring Symbio 700<\/strong><\/h2><p>Odyssey condensers can be paired with many different Odyssey AHUs, 3rd party AHUs, and indoor coil assemblies. Due to this, Symbio 700 must be configured for the connected AHU, installed accessories, and the controlling sensor/thermostat. <\/p><h3>Configuring Link from Trane Commercial Help Center<\/h3><h4>Phone Application <\/h4><ul><li><p><a href=\"#a/16561370756237\">Symbio™ 700 - Precedent or Odyssey - How to configure or edit configurations using the Mobile Phone App<\/a><\/p><\/li><\/ul><h4>Onboard Display<\/h4><ul><li><a href=\"#a/43591585514509\">https://support.trane.com/hc/en-us/articles/43591585514509-Symbio-700-How-to-use-the-onboard-display-to-Configure-or-Edit-Configuration<\/a><\/li><\/ul><h4>Tracer TU<\/h4><ul><li><a href=\"#a/22501920613773\">https://support.trane.com/hc/en-us/articles/22501920613773-Symbio-700-Using-Trane-Tracer-TU-to-edit-or-configure<\/a><\/li><\/ul><h3>VVZT Configuration Needed<\/h3><p>For <strong>Variable Volume<\/strong> Zone Temperature (VVZT or formerly known as SZVAV) operation, set the configuration as follows (requires zone sensor):<\/p><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>System Type<\/strong> = VVZT (requires zone sensor for actual VVZT operation, tstat will operate as CVZT)<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Efficiency<\/strong> = Standard B<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Indoor Fan Type <\/strong>= Variable Speed (should set automatically)<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Space Controller<\/strong> = Single Setpoint Zone sensor, or Dual Setpoint Zone Sensor<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Frostat<\/strong> = Installed (should install automatically) + (On Odyssey, this is the high limit for the VFD)<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Discharge Temperature Sensor<\/strong> = Installed (should install automatically)<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Field-installed electric heat <span>only<\/span><\/strong><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>TTA condenser<\/strong><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Source<\/strong> = Electric<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Stages<\/strong> = 1 or 2 (depending on electric heat kit stages)<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>TWA condenser<\/strong><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Source<\/strong> = Heat Pump<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Stages<\/strong> = 1 or 2<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Secondary Heating Source<\/strong> = Electric<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Secondary Heating Stages<\/strong> = 1 or 2 (depending on electric heat kit stages)<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<\/ul><h3>Multispeed Configuration Needed<\/h3><p>For <strong>Multispeed<\/strong> fan operation, set the configuration as follows:<\/p><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>System Type<\/strong> = CVZT (thermostat or zone sensor)<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Efficiency<\/strong> = Standard B<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Indoor Fan Type<\/strong> = Multi Speed<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Space Controller<\/strong> = Conventional Thermostat, Single Setpoint Zone sensor, or Dual Setpoint Zone Sensor<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Frostat<\/strong> = Installed (On Odyssey, this is the high limit for the VFD)<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Discharge Temperature Sensor<\/strong> = Installed (with zone sensor, will use DAT logic for compressor staging) or Not Installed for conventional thermostat.<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Field-installed electric heat <span>only<\/span><\/strong><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>TTA condenser<\/strong><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Source<\/strong> = Electric<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Stages<\/strong> = 1 or 2 (depending on electric heat kit stages)<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>TWA condenser<\/strong><ul>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Source<\/strong> = Heat Pump<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Primary Heating Stages<\/strong> = 1 or 2<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Secondary Heating Source<\/strong> = Electric<\/li>\n<li>\n<strong>Secondary Heating Stages<\/strong> = 1 or 2 (depending on electric heat kit stages)<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<\/ul><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical product. Field installed smoke detectors should be installed in the supply/return ducts connected to the unit and wired back to the unit E-Stop terminals:<\/li>\n<li>ReliaTel equipment at LTB-5 &amp; 6 ( <a href=\"https://support.trane.com/hc/en-us/articles/360037491412\">Where do I wire my field installed smoke detector?<\/a> )<\/li>\n<li>Symbio700 equipment at J18-1 &amp; 2 ( <a href=\"#a/360055257251\">Symbio™ 700 - Emergency Stop or Equipment Shutdown Input<\/a> )<\/li>\n<\/ul><h2>Common Smoke Detector Questions<\/h2><zd-accordion><details><summary>Do factory installed smoke detectors communicate?<\/summary><div><ul><li>No, factory installed detectors do not communicate.<\/li><\/ul><\/div><\/details><details><summary>Where does the fire alarm company wire to the factory mounted return/supply smoke detector?<\/summary><div><ul>\n<li>In the factory supply/return detector, terminals 6 &amp; 17 are used for the alarm notification back to the unit controls (RTOM on ReliaTel units and FAOM/IOM on Symbio units) and should NOT be used by the fire alarm company. Doing so usually creates an alarm at the unit and shuts it down.<\/li>\n<li>The detector has another set of SPDT (7 = NC, 8 = NO, 18 = C) contacts for alarm notification and a set of NO (13 = NO, 14 = C) contacts for fire alarm control panel connection, see attachment at the bottom of this article for the smoke detector wiring diagram.<\/li>\n<li>For more information about the factory installed supply/return smoke detectors, please contact the manufacturer: Air Products &amp; Controls, 888-332-2241<\/li>\n<\/ul><\/div><\/details><details><summary>Where does the fire alarm company wire to the factory mounted plenum smoke detector?<\/summary><div><ul><li>Please contact the manufacturer: Air Products &amp; Controls, 888-332-2241<\/li><\/ul><\/div><\/details><details><summary>We need to power the factory installed smoke detector from a separate source to avoid nuisance alarms at the fire panel during unit maintenance, how do we do this?<\/summary><div><ul>\n<li>Technical Support does not recommend doing this as it requires cutting the factory harness, requires wiring modifications at the detector and may affect the UL listing of the unit.<\/li>\n<li>Factory detectors do not communicate and would not produce a communications or trouble fault at the panel if powered down.<\/li>\n<li>The FACP should be monitoring the status of the dry contacts only, these will not change when powering the smoke detector down unless otherwise noted in the installation instructions.<\/li>\n<li>If this still does not meet the requirements for the project, install field mounted detectors in the ductwork. <\/li>\n<\/ul><\/div><\/details><details><summary>What is the part number for the kit to field install the factory smoke detectors?<\/summary><div><ul>\n<li>We do not offer a kit to install smoke detectors in units that were not built with them.<\/li>\n<li>In this scenario, a field mounted detector should be used mounted in the duct work and wired back to the unit emergency stop.<\/li>\n<\/ul><\/div><\/details><\/zd-accordion><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. Safety / Emergency Stop; Manuals / Literature","Troubleshooting","Light Commercial Rooftops | Splits","2025-10-23","2026-10-14","<h2>Compressor Proving Troubleshooting<\/h2><p>Are you looking for this Symbio 700 Compressor Proving Circuit Troubleshooting for Precedent and Large Foundation? For units with ReliaTel controls (RTRM), see the <a href=\"https://support.trane.com/hc/en-us/articles/42150055236109\">ReliaTel Compressor Proving article<\/a>, and for units with a Symbio 700 unit controller for Odyssey (TTA or TWA), see the <a href=\"#a/39347684774541\">Odyssey Compressor Proving article<\/a>.<\/p><p>This troubleshooting guide applies to Symbio 700 unit controllers on Precedent and Large Foundation units with model number prefixes: Precedent - DHJ, DHK, DSJ, DSK, THJ, THK, TSJ, TSK, TZK, WHJ, WHK, WSJ, WSK, YHJ, YHK, YSJ, YSK, and YZK, and Large Foundation - EDK and GDK. Use it when diagnosing compressor 1 proving, compressor 2 proving, compressor proving trip, compressor proving lockout, and compressor contactor fail alarms. It covers compressor will not start, contactor not pulling in, proving circuit open, relay contact resistance, auxiliary switch checks, and the exact connector and wire paths used to verify the circuit.<\/p><zd-accordion>\n  <details>\n    <summary>Symptoms and alarm codes<\/summary>\n    <div>\n      <p>\n        Use this section to match the alarm behavior to the likely fault.\n      <\/p>\n      <ul>\n        <li>\n          <strong>Alarm - Compressor Proving Trip Diagnostic – 15-minute lockout<\/strong>:\n          The HPC or DTL opens while the compressor is running after\n          the\n          circuit has proved, and the Compressor Contactor loses its\n          24\n          VAC, causing the Aux Switch to open.\n        <\/li>\n        <li>\n          <strong>Alarm - Compressor Proving Lockout Diagnostic – INSTANT UNIT LOCKOUT!<\/strong>:\n          The Symbio Board calls for the Compressor Contactor, and\n          the\n          Contactor doesn't pull in within 7 seconds.\n        <\/li>\n        <li>\n          <strong>Alarm - Compressor Proving Lockout Diagnostic – INSTANT UNIT LOCKOUT!<\/strong>:\n          The Symbio Board calls for the Compressor Contactor; it pulls\n          in and drops back out after 7 seconds.\n        <\/li>\n        <li>\n          <strong>Alarm - Compressor Contactor Fail Diagnostic – INSTANT UNIT LOCKOUT!<\/strong>:\n          The Compressor Contactor Aux Switch closes before it is supposed\n          to.\n        <\/li>\n      <\/ul>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Possible causes:<\/strong> HPC and/or DTL open, contactor\n        coil open, wiring/connection error, no 24 VAC at the black wire\n        on\n        the Aux Switch, bad Aux Switch, Aux Switch not on the contactor\n        correctly,\n        technician manually pushes in the contactor, or the contactor\n        is\n        welded shut.\n      <\/p>\n    <\/div>\n  <\/details>\n  <details>\n    <summary>Abbreviation Names<\/summary>\n    <div>\n        <div>\n          <h2>Symbio 700 Compressor Proving Troubleshooter<\/h2>\n          <p>\n            Precedent &amp; Large Foundation — interactive companion\n            to the Compressor\n            Proving Circuit article\n          <\/p>\n        <\/div>\n        <div>\n          <div><\/div>\n          <div><\/div>\n          <div>\n            <span><\/span><button <div>\n      <p>An Important verification that is often overlooked<\/p>\n      <div>\n<p>If the HPC or DTL opened while the compressor was already running and the circuit had previously proved, do <strong>not<\/strong>assume the fault is only in the proving wiring, voltage, resistance, or board issue. This type of trip can also indicate a <strong>refrigerant-system problem, such as incorrect charge, restricted airflow, condenser issues, or another condition that causes<\/strong> abnormal head pressure or temperature. <strong>Power down the unit and unplug the compressor. <\/strong>Isolate power, unplug the compressor, and wait about 15 minutes before determining the next step.<\/p>\n<p>If the fault does not return quickly while the compressor is unplugged, focus first on troubleshooting the refrigerant circuit rather than assuming a wiring, voltage, resistance, or board issue. If you find no refrigerant-side issue, continue below to test the Compressor Proving wiring<strong>.<\/strong><\/p>\n<\/div>\n    <\/div>\n  <\/div>\n<\/aside><h3>Compressor 1<\/h3><zd-accordion><details><summary>Compressor 1: 24VAC Voltage Tests<\/summary><div>\n<p><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2728\">Verify if 24VAC control power is present at the Unit Controller.<\/span><\/p>\n<ul>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"2800\"><strong>Setup:<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2808\"> Unit powered <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2824\"><strong>ON<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2828\">. Set your multimeter to <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2855\"><strong>VAC<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2860\">.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"2868\"><strong>Reference Point:<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2886\"> Place your black meter lead on clean <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2926\"><strong>Chassis Ground<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"2942\">.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<p><img loading=\"lazy\" J20-9 &gt; UC-J9-3<\/span><\/p>\n<\/div><\/details><\/zd-accordion><h3><span data-markdown-start-index=\"14947\">Board Diagnostic: Relay Contact Resistance Check<\/span><\/h3><p><span data-markdown-start-index=\"14999\">If you suspect the Symbio 700 board's internal compressor relays are damaged (pitted or sticking), perform this manual test using your phone's service app.<\/span><\/p><zd-accordion><details><summary><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15691\">Compressor 1 (CC1 Relay) Check Steps<\/span><\/summary><div>\n<ol>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"15735\"><strong>Unplug<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15743\"> the <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15750\"><strong>UC-J8<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15757\"> harness from the <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15777\"><strong>Red P8<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15785\"> connector on the Symbio board.<\/span><ul><li>\n<em><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15823\">Take note:<\/span><\/em><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15834\"> Orange wire is <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15852\"><strong>UC-P8-4<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15861\">, Yellow wire is <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15880\"><strong>UC-P8-5<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15889\">.<\/span>\n<\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"15893\">Connect to the board with the Symbio Mobile App. Go to <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15950\"><strong>Tools<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15957\"> &gt; <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15967\"><strong>Service Test Mode<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"15986\">.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"15986\">Force initiate <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16003\"><strong>Cool 2<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16011\"> in the app to energize the relay. <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16048\"><strong>LED 5<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16055\"> on the board must turn <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16081\"><strong>ON<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16085\">.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16089\">Measure <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16099\"><strong>Resistance (Ω)<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16116\"> directly across board pins <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16146\"><strong>P8-4<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16152\"> and <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16159\"><strong>P8-5<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16165\">.<\/span><ul><li><em><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16171\">You only have a 5-second window to read this.<\/span><\/em><\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16266\"><strong>Specification:<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16282\"> Must be <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16293\"><strong>&lt; 10 Ω<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16302\">. Repeat this test 3–4 times. If it ever reads above 10 ohms, replace the board.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055248371_31369962357773_mceclip5.png\" width=\"609\" height=\"229\"><\/p>\n<p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055248371_39023444760077_P8.jpg\" width=\"3024\" height=\"4032\"><\/p>\n<\/div><\/details><details><summary><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16386\">Compressor 2 (CC2 Relay) Check Steps<\/span><\/summary><div>\n<ol>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16430\"><strong>Unplug<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16438\"> the <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16445\"><strong>UC-J9<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16452\"> harness from the <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16472\"><strong>White UC-P9<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16485\"> connector on the Symbio board.<\/span><ul><li>\n<em><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16522\">Take note:<\/span><\/em><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16533\"> Yellow/Black wire is <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16557\"><strong>UC-P9-4<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16566\">, Brown/Black wire is <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16590\"><strong>UC-P9-5<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16599\">.<\/span>\n<\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16603\">Connect to the board with the Symbio Mobile App. Go to <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16660\"><strong>Tools<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16667\"> &gt; <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16677\"><strong>Service Test Mode<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16696\">.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16696\">Force initiate <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16713\"><strong>Cool 1<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16721\"> in the app to energize the relay. <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16758\"><strong>LED 15<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16766\"> on the board must turn <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16792\"><strong>ON<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16796\">.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16800\">Measure <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16810\"><strong>Resistance (Ω)<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16827\"> directly across board pins <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16857\"><strong>P9-4<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16863\"> and <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16870\"><strong>P9-5<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16876\">.<\/span><ul><li><em><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16882\">You only have a 5-second window to read this.<\/span><\/em><\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li>\n<li>\n<span data-markdown-start-index=\"16977\"><strong>Specification:<\/strong><\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"16993\"> Must be <\/span><span data-markdown-start-index=\"17004\"><strong>&lt; If it ever reads above 10 ohms, replace the board.<\/span>\n<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055248371_31369975260429_mceclip4.png\" width=\"633\" height=\"234\"><\/p>\n<p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/360055248371_39023430046093_P9.jpg\" width=\"3024\" height=\"4032\"><\/p>\n\n<\/div><\/details><\/zd-accordion><aside>\n  <div>\n    <i aria-hidden=\"true\"><\/i>\n    <div>\n      <p>\n        Complete the troubleshooting outlined above before replacing any\n        parts.\n      <\/p>\n      <div>\n        <p>\n          For confirmed Symbio 700 relay failures, replace the Symbio 700\n          and Adapter board using the kits listed below; see the matrix\n          for kit number vs. product.\n        <\/p>\n        <figure>\n          <table>\n            <colgroup>\n              <col>\n              <col>\n              <col>\n            <\/colgroup>\n            <thead>\n              <tr>\n                <th>Product<\/th>\n                <th>Symbio 700 License Type<\/th>\n                <th>\n                  <strong>Kit Part #<\/strong>\n                <\/th>\n              <\/tr>\n            <\/thead>\n            <tbody>\n              <tr>\n                <td rowspan=\"2\">\n                  <p>Precedent<\/p>\n                <\/td>\n                <td>\n                  <p>Advanced<\/p>\n                <\/td>\n                <td>\n                  <p>KIT20878 (Incl. By using multiple sensors, the average temperature can be sensed and provide operation more acceptable for the zone.<\/p><p>The quantity of sensors in the sensing circuit is extremely important. The quantity must be a \"squared\" number that allows them to be wired in series-parallel configuration. The resistance of the averaging circuit must duplicate the resistance of a single sensor. The quantities in the averaging circuit will be 4, 9, 16 or etc. A 4 sensor circuit is typical. (Note: Operation with 2 or 3 sensors is not possible).<\/p><p>Space temperature averaging is accomplished by wiring the correct number of sensors as shown. The fewest number of sensors required to accomplish space temperature averaging is four. See note below.<\/p><p><strong>NOTE:  A non-programmable sensor can be used as one of the sensors wired in series/parallel with the others. A programmable sensor can not be used as one of the averaging sensors<\/strong>.<\/p><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/4743220801549_4743120873357_mceclip0.png\" alt=\"mceclip0.png\"><\/p><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/4743220801549_4743075371277_mceclip1.png\" alt=\"mceclip1.png\"><\/p><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/4743220801549_4743019595021_mceclip2.png\" alt=\"mceclip3.png\"><\/p><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. III with Symbio 700 Controls Compressor Proving Alarms","Voyager","Compressor / Compressor Proving; Wiring / Wire Pairing; Troubleshooting; Installation / Startup; Heating; Safety / Emergency Stop","V3 w/Symbio 700 controls","Large Commercial RTUs | Splits | CSC","2024-07-24","2026-10-07","<div>\n  <a href=\"https://support.trane.com/hc/en-us/requests/new?ticket_form_id=40062320160013&amp;tf_subject=Symbio+700+V3+-+Compressor+Related+Issues+%28Compressor+Proving%29&amp;subject=Symbio+700+V3+-+Compressor+Related+Issues+%28Compressor+Proving%29&amp;request%5Bsubject%5D=Symbio+700+V3+-+Compressor+Related+Issues+%28Compressor+Proving%29&amp;tf_1900001159007=2547882&amp;request%5Bcustom_fields%5D%5B1900001159007%5D=2547882&amp;tf_360033238112=prod_largecomm&amp;request%5Bcustom_fields%5D%5B360033238112%5D=prod_largecomm\">Report an instance of this problem<\/a>\n<\/div><p>This diagnostic is created when the unit calls for a compressor contractor to energize, and the contactor does not pull in or drops out during operation.  This would normally indicate that a safety (HPC or DTL) has opened, but anything that causes the aux contacts on the contactor to show as open when they should be closed will generate this diagnostic.<\/p><p>If the contactor does not pull in when requested, check wire connections.  Poor wire connections in the wire harness seem to be the biggest issue we are seeing, primarily the two pin harness connectors for the HPC's, DTL's, and the compressor contactor coils.  (See attached schematic).  These connectors are only there for ease of assembly purposes.  We have suggested removing them and using wire nuts or splices to correct the issue as a field fix.  In many cases, this solves the problem. <\/p><p>Other, less obvious, causes can be a tripped transformer or poor harness connection on the Adapter Board.  Compressor Contactors 1 and 2 get their 24 VAC power from the transformer connected to J25 on the Adapter Board.  Compressor Contactor 3 gets its 24 VAC power from the transformer connected to J8 on the Adapter Board.  Verify that these two connectors on the Adapter Board are receiving 24 VAC.  If either of these transformers have tripped, failed, or have a poor harness connection the unit will generate this diagnostic when it calls for the compressor to start. <\/p><p>It is also possible to have a bad aux switch on the compressor contact.  If the contactor pulls in, but drops out a few seconds later, check the proving switch and wiring.<\/p><p><strong>A test that can be performed is:<\/strong><\/p><ol>\n<li><p>Power down the unit/verify that there is no voltage present on the compressor contactors<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Remove the fuses for the compressor or Load wiring to the contactor of the compressor you're troubleshooting (in this example we'll cover CC1/Compressor 1 proving).<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Insert a jumper into the P8 plug between pins 4 &amp; 5 (Orange and Yellow wires...see snip below from the schematic).<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Power on the unit and verify that the CC1 contactor pulls in and you have 24Vac present on P8 pin 3  (blue wire) for the proving input...if the CC1 does not pull in then there could be a lack of 24Vac, loose connection, or an open safety as mentioned above. If the CC1 does pull in and you do have 24Vac on pin 3 of P8 and you continue to get Compressor 1 Proving diagnostics then there could be an issue with the Symbio 700. You can repeat/copy these steps for CC2 and CC3 (Compressor 2 is the P9 and Compressor 3 is the P10). <strong>If everything is working properly the Symbio should trigger a \"Compressor X Contactor Failure\" diagnostic.<\/strong><\/p><\/li>\n<\/ol><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/28681362710413_36062195650445_mceclip0.png\"><\/p><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/28681362710413_28686999695117_Adaptor_Board.png\" alt=\"Adaptor Board.png\"><\/p><p><span><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/span><\/p><p><span>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. Any attempt to install, service, or repair a heating or central air conditioning product may result in personal injury and/or property damage. Troubleshooting; Firmware / Software; Sensors / Modules; Communications / BACnet; Installation / Startup; Heating; Manuals / Literature","Precedent","Equipment Literature | Apps Guides","2023-05-03","2026-10-05","<h1><strong>Precedent - Application Guide<\/strong><\/h1><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/15455235169677_40304510667149_mceclip0.png\" width=\"386\" height=\"444\"><\/p><p>Click the e-library symbol for the latest version of the <strong>Symbio Precedent Application Guide<\/strong>.<\/p><p data-darkreader-inline-color=\"\" data-darkreader-inline-bgcolor=\"\"><a href=\"https://elibrary.tranetechnologies.com/commercial/search?maxFacetValues=300&amp;n=&amp;term=publicationID_md%3AACC-APG002*\" data-darkreader-inline-color=\"\" data-darkreader-inline-bgcolor=\"\"><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/12103145325837_27087515957389_mceclip0.png\"><\/span><\/a><\/p><p>Or <strong>download<\/strong> at the end of the warning sign.<\/p><h2>Introduction<\/h2><p>The Symbio™ 700 installed on Precedent™ rooftop units is a factory-installed and programmed controller, providing digital control and protection of the equipment.<\/p><p>The Symbio 700 has two model options:<\/p><ul>\n<li><p><strong>Standard Controller<\/strong> — provides advanced troubleshooting and access to the Symbio Service and Installation mobile app via a Bluetooth connection. The Standard controller does not support remote communications of any type or custom TGP2 programming abilities. A Standard controller can be converted to an Advanced controller<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p><strong>Advanced Controller<\/strong> — supports Building Automation System interface via BACnet® (ANSI/ASHRAE Standard 135- 2016), Modbus™, Air-Fi®, or LonTalk®. The Advanced Controller also allows optional (field applied) TGP2 programming utilizing optional XM30 and XM32 Expansion Module points. The advanced controller is a purchased feature.<\/p><\/li>\n<\/ul><p>The Symbio 700 offers multiple equipment configuration options regardless of controller model. The Precedent rooftop unit can be configured as the following system types:<\/p><ul>\n<li><p>Conventional Thermostat Control (T-Stat) - Heating and cooling operation for space comfort managed by conventional thermostat inputs.<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Constant Volume Zone Temperature Control (CVZT) - Multi-speed supply fan control to manage space comfort.<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Variable Volume Zone Temperature Control (VVZT) - The modulating supply fan speed modulates with heating or cooling capacity to maintain space comfort.<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Variable Volume Discharge Air (VVDA) - The supply fan modulates air volume to maintain duct static pressure control. Also known as Multi-Zone VAV.<\/p><\/li>\n<\/ul><p>These configurations can be used with standard cooling systems.<\/p><p>This guide provides information about the configuration, control capabilities and troubleshooting of the Precedent system with Symbio 700 controller.<\/p><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. Any attempt to install, service, or repair a heating or central air conditioning product may result in personal injury and/or property damage. Firmware / Software; Communications / BACnet; Installation / Startup; Heating; Safety / Emergency Stop; Manuals / Literature","General","Light Commercial Rooftops | Splits","2023-03-16","2026-09-16","<h2>Summary:<\/h2><p>The Trane Symbio 700 equipment controller pairs with the Symbio mobile application using Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE). When paired with the Symbio 700 controller, technicians can use the Symbio mobile application to edit operating setpoints, adjust other parameters and settings, and perform a range of service and troubleshooting tasks while standing at the unit.<\/p><div><div><p>Make sure you give the app full permissions; if not, the phone app won't connect with the Symbio Unit Controller.<\/p><\/div><\/div><p><br> <\/p><h3>Needed to connect to the Symbio 700 Bluetooth<\/h3><p>• Smart devices supported:<br>   – iPhone®<br>   – Android™<br>• Trane Symbio Service &amp; Installation mobile app<\/p><h3>Connecting to the Symbio 700 Controller<\/h3><p><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/14059563735565_14058374124173_download.jfif\" alt=\"download.jfif\" width=\"237\" height=\"212\"><\/p><ol>\n<li>Enable Bluetooth on your smart device.<\/li>\n<li>Access the Symbio 700 unit controller in the low-voltage section of the equipment.<\/li>\n<li>Press on the Symbio 700 keyboard/display to turn on Bluetooth.<\/li>\n<li>\n<p>Confirm the status of Bluetooth communications.<br><strong>Blue LED Display Description<\/strong><\/p>\n<figure><table>\n<thead><tr>\n<th><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/14059563735565_48542928122893_image.png\" width=\"43\" height=\"43\"><\/figure><\/th>\n<th>Blue LED<\/th>\n<th>Display<\/th>\n<th>Description<\/th>\n<\/tr><\/thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td rowspan=\"3\"><p>Press for On/Off<\/p><\/td>\n<td>Off<\/td>\n<td>NOT CONNECTED<\/td>\n<td>Bluetooth Off<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Blinking<\/td>\n<td>WAITING<\/td>\n<td>Bluetooth On — Not Paired<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>On Solid<\/td>\n<td>CONNECTED<\/td>\n<td>Bluetooth On — Connected/Paired<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<\/tbody>\n<\/table><\/figure><p><strong>Symbio 700 Bluetooth status<\/strong><br><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/14059563735565_14058573459853_Bluetooth_LED_Status.png\" alt=\"Bluetooth_LED_Status.png\" width=\"278\" height=\"238\"><\/p>\n<\/li>\n<li>Start the mobile app on your smart device.<\/li>\n<li>On the login screen, press [View Available Devices] in the lower portion of the screen. Or Trane personnel can log in using their Trane Connect username and password.<\/li>\n<li>\n<p>On the Unit List page, select the Symbio 700 controller to pair with. If the controller is not listed, press the refresh arrow in the upper right-hand corner of the screen.<\/p>\n<aside>\n  <div>\n    <i aria-hidden=\"true\"><\/i>\n    <div>\n      <p>Note<\/p>\n      <div><p>If a Symbio 700 is not the original Symbio controller as shipped with the equipment, the Bluetooth equipment list will list the controller serial number, instead of the equipment serial number.<\/p><\/div>\n    <\/div>\n  <\/div>\n<\/aside>\n<\/li>\n<li>When prompted, pair the app to the Symbio 700 controller. A popup message displays a 6-digit<br>random number. The same number is shown on the display of the Symbio 700 controller until the<br>pairing is complete, allowing the user to confirm the connection to the intended controller.<br><br><strong>Bluetooth pairing<\/strong><br><strong><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/14059563735565_14058747394957_pairing_message.png\" alt=\"pairing_message.png\" width=\"367\" height=\"158\"><\/strong>\n<\/li>\n<li>Press [Check Mark - <strong>√<\/strong>] on the Symbio 700 onboard keyboard/display to complete the pairing.<br>When the LED light is a solid blue and the display reads Bluetooth Connected, the Bluetooth pairing and<br>connection is complete.<\/li>\n<\/ol><h3>Troubleshooting<\/h3><figure><table>\n<thead><tr>\n<th>Issue<\/th>\n<th>Description<\/th>\n<\/tr><\/thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Smart device requirements not met<\/td>\n<td>\n<ul>\n<li>Apple iPhone (iOS v10; iPhone 6 or later required)<\/li>\n<li>Android (v5.0 Lollipop or later; a device with Bluetooth v4.2 or later required)<\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<aside>\n  <div>\n    <i aria-hidden=\"true\"><\/i>\n    <div>\n      <p>Note<\/p>\n      <div><p>It is not possible to check what Bluetooth connectivity version is installed on an Android device, as it does not appear in Settings.<strong>﻿<\/strong><\/p><\/div>\n    <\/div>\n  <\/div>\n<\/aside>\n<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Multiple users attempting to connect to the controller via Bluetooth<\/td>\n<td>\n<p>Only one user can connect to the Symbio 700 controller via Bluetooth.<\/p>\n<p>If the blue light is solid, another user is connected to the controller.<\/p>\n<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Device outside of Bluetooth range limits<\/td>\n<td>Bluetooth has physical range limitations. A user can lose connectivity if too far away from the controller and will need to re-access connectivity from the Units List page. <\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Exceeded limit of equipment pairings<\/td>\n<td>\n<p>The app only allows 10 saved pairings per device. <\/p>\n<ul>\n<li>Android devices will auto delete the oldest pairing. If auto delete fails, you can manually deleted pairings. <\/li>\n<li>iOS users must manually delete pairing through Settings. <\/li>\n<\/ul>\n<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Smart device unpaired or disconnected from controller<\/td>\n<td><ul>\n<li>Pressing the Bluetooth button on the controller while connected will disconnect the controller from the smart device.<\/li>\n<li>Cycling power to the controller, a firmware update, or a restore/start controller will disconnect the Bluetooth connection. <\/li>\n<li>Equipment shutdown will disconnected from Bluetooth connection.<\/li>\n<\/ul><\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<\/tbody>\n<\/table><\/figure><h3>Bluetooth Operation<\/h3><p>Bluetooth is a short-range wireless communication technology that allows electronic devices to communicate without the need for wires or cables. The Symbio 700 controller uses Bluetooth to communicate with the Symbio Service and Installation Mobile App for initial startup and servicing. For more information about the Symbio 700 Bluetooth download the pdf below.<\/p><p>Below is an overview of the expected Bluetooth operation on the Symbio 700.<\/p><figure><table>\n<thead><tr>\n<th><strong>Action<\/strong><\/th>\n<th><strong>Outcome<\/strong><\/th>\n<\/tr><\/thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Pressing the Bluetooth button on the Symbio 700 controller when the blue LED is off.<\/td>\n<td>Starts the Bluetooth radio for pairing for 5 minutes. The blue LED will start flashing on the controller signaling the radio is ready for connection. If a device is not paired within the 5-minute window the Bluetooth radio is turned off, and the button must be pressed again to restart the process. If a device is successfully connected within the 5-minute window the blue LED will turn solid. <\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Pressing the Bluetooth button on the Symbio 700 when the blue LED is already flashing or solid. <\/td>\n<td>The Bluetooth radio is turned off. The blue LED will turn off.<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>No activity on the Mobile app for 1 hour while connected via Bluetooth. <\/td>\n<td>The Symbio 700 controller will automatically turn off the Bluetooth radio after 1 hour of no activity. The blue LED will turn off. This will break the connection to the mobile app, and reconnecting will be required. <\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<tr>\n<td>Walking away from the Symbio 700 controller while connected via Bluetooth. <\/td>\n<td>If you have a direct line of sight to the controller the Bluetooth range is approximately 25 feet. Without a direct line of sight to the controller, the range will vary depending on the type of obstructions. If you are outside of the Bluetooth range, the connection will be lost, and reconnecting will be required. The blue LED will be flashing when the connection is lost to the mobile app.<\/td>\n<\/tr>\n<\/tbody>\n<\/table><\/figure><p>Click the e-library symbol for the latest version<\/p><p><a href=\"https://elibrary.tranetechnologies.com/commercial/search?maxFacetValues=12&amp;n=&amp;term=publicationID_md%3ABAS-SLM070*&amp;f.isCurrentDisplay_md=Current&amp;f.publicationLanguageDisplay_md=English+%5Ben%5D&amp;viewPDF=true\"><span><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/12103145325837_27087515957389_mceclip0.png\"><\/span><\/a><\/p><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. The Standard controller does not support remote communications of any type or custom TGP2 programming abilities. A Standard controller can be converted to an Advanced controller<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p><strong>Advanced Controller<\/strong> — supports Building Automation System interface via BACnet® (ANSI/ASHRAE Standard 135- 2016), Modbus™, Air-Fi®, or LonTalk®. The Advanced Controller also allows optional (field applied) TGP2 programming utilizing optional XM30 and XM32 Expansion Module points. The advanced controller is a purchased feature.<\/p><\/li>\n<\/ul><p>The Symbio 700 offers multiple equipment configuration options regardless of controller model. The Precedent rooftop unit can be configured as the following system types:<\/p><ul>\n<li><p>Conventional Thermostat Control (T-Stat) - Heating and cooling operation for space comfort managed by conventional thermostat inputs.<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Constant Volume Zone Temperature Control (CVZT) - Multi-speed supply fan control to manage space comfort.<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Variable Volume Zone Temperature Control (VVZT) - The modulating supply fan speed modulates with heating or cooling capacity to maintain space comfort.<\/p><\/li>\n<li><p>Variable Volume Discharge Air (VVDA) - The supply fan modulates air volume to maintain duct static pressure control. Also known as Multi-Zone VAV.<\/p><\/li>\n<\/ul><p>These configurations can be used with standard cooling systems.<\/p><p>This guide provides information about the configuration, control capabilities and troubleshooting of the Precedent system with Symbio 700 controller.<\/p><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. Any attempt to install, service, or repair a heating or central air conditioning product may result in personal injury and/or property damage. BACnet Out-of-Service control scheme","General Symbio 700","Troubleshooting; Communications / BACnet; Installation / Startup; Heating","Applies to all Symbio 800 Controlled Units","Large Commercial RTUs | Splits | CSC","2023-05-24","2026-06-18","<p>On Reliatel and IntelliPak Legacy (IntelliPak 1 and 2) units with BACnet, the out-of-service functionality was used to add and remove BAS points from the arbitration scheme. This implementation was unique to Trane and did not meet the BACnet standard’s intent for “Out-of-Service”.  The BACnet standard’s intent was to use “Out-of-Service” as a troubleshooting and servicing feature.  This is and will NOT be the case on the newer Symbio controlled units. <\/p>\n<p>On the newer Symbio 700/800 controllers when placing a point out-of-service it will continue to be used in the arbitration scheme and use the fixed value assigned by the technician.  “Out-of-Service” should not be used to clear point faults in the controller as the Symbio 700/800 controllers rely on point faulting to determine validity of the point.  If a point is placed “Out-of-Service” to eliminate a point fault,  the controller will think the point is valid and continue to use the out-of-service value assigned by the technician indefinitely.  The Symbio 700/800 controllers use an entirely different style of arbitration that does not rely on the out-of-service feature.<\/p>\n<p>On Symbio 800 controllers, technicians can delete BACnet points from the interface if it will not be used for unit operation.  This will remove the point from the arbitration scheme and places it into a “Recycle Bin”.  The intent for this feature is to remove unnecessary BACnet points from the interface to make it easier for 3<sup>rd<\/sup> party integrators to understand our equipment.<\/p>\n<p><strong>Instructions on how to Delete and Recycle Points<\/strong><\/p>\n<p>Deleting and recycling points can be done with TU.  The instructions below show steps for accomplishing both processes with Symbio UI after direct connecting to the controller and typing <strong><a href=\"https://hub.tranetechnologies.com/external-link.jspa?url=http%3A%2F%2F198.80.18.1\">http://198.80.18.1<\/a><\/strong> into the web browser. <\/p>\n<p><strong> <\/strong><\/p>\n<p><strong>Deleting Points<\/strong><\/p>\n<p>Deleting points is convenient for removing unwanted data from the controller communication<\/p>\n<p>interface. The point is not permanently deleted; rather the point is simply moved to <strong>Recycled<\/strong><\/p>\n<p><strong>Points<\/strong>.<\/p>\n<p>All point overrides, priority array owners, and status are reset to factory settings.<\/p>\n<p>To delete a point:<\/p>\n<ol>\n<li>Click <strong>Points <\/strong>in left navigation pane.<\/li>\n<li>Navigate to Analog, Binary, or Multistate points and select the point(s) using check box left of<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<p>name.<\/p>\n<ol start=\"3\">\n<li>Select <strong>Actions <\/strong>pull-down menu, then <strong>Delete <\/strong>&gt; <strong>Yes – Delete <\/strong>to confirm the action. <strong>No – Cancel<\/strong>\n<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<p>to cancel action.<\/p>\n<p><strong>Recycled Points<\/strong><\/p>\n<p>Points that have been deleted from the controller interface are moved to Recycled Points. In this<\/p>\n<p>location the points can be restored to the controller interface and used once again to communicate<\/p>\n<p>data via BACnet®, MODBUS® or LonTalk®.<\/p>\n<p>To view recycled points and restore points:<\/p>\n<ol>\n<li>Click <strong>Points <\/strong>in left navigation pane.<\/li>\n<li>Click <strong>Recycled Points <\/strong>to view all deleted points.<\/li>\n<li>To resort a point, click the box left of the point name, then click <strong>Restore<\/strong>.<\/li>\n<\/ol>\n<p><strong>Please see attachment for Deleting and Recycling Points with TU. The Screen snips from the Preso is below.<\/strong><\/p>\n<p><strong><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/16077877900173_39608119669389_mceclip0.png\"><\/strong><\/p>\n<p><strong><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/16077877900173_39608119672845_mceclip1.png\"><\/strong><\/p>\n\n\n<p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p>\n<p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. VFD / Drives; Troubleshooting; Installation / Startup; Heating; Manuals / Literature","Airflow / Motors / Fans [LCU]","Light Commercial Rooftops | Splits","2023-05-22","2026-06-02","<h2>Background<\/h2><p>When replacing the VFD within the air handler, it is important to program it correctly. A new drive from Trane Parts will not come preprogrammed for the specific unit. The choice of which set of parameters you use is based on the air handler model number. <\/p><ul><li>A model with the 7th digit = K will use the Odyssey A2L R454B VFD Parameters. <ul><li>For example: TWE120K3AAA07BD4000000000000000000000000<\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li><\/ul><ul><li>A model with the 14th digit = B will use the Odyssey model 14th digit = B VFD Parameters<ul><li>For example: TWE18043BAA07BC0000000000000000000000000<\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li><\/ul><ul><li>A model with the 14th digit = A will use the Odyssey model 14th digit = A VFD Parameters<ul><li>For example: TWE07244BAA05AD0000000000000000000000000<\/li><\/ul>\n<\/li><\/ul><p>For assistance on how to navigate the parameters in the drive for programming, see the article <a href=\"#a/36072951827853\">Programming or changing parameters in the TR150 VFD<\/a><\/p><h2>Quick Transfer of Parameter Setting from the old VFD to the replacement VFD<\/h2><p>For a drive that will still power the keypad, it may be faster to transfer the parameter settings with the keypad. The data can be saved to the keypad before the VFD is replaced and then transferred to the replacement VFD.<\/p><p>If the failed drive has no power and it is not able to save the parameters to the keypad, it is recommended once manually installing the parameters in the new drive to save the data to the keypad for any future issues.<\/p><p>If the replacement VFD is having the same issues as the old VFD after data transfer, there may be a parameter outside the replacement list that was changed and copied over to the new drive. For this instance, it may be necessary to restore the<a href=\"https://support.trane.com/hc/en-us/articles/5215124420749\"> VFD to factory defaults<\/a> and manually install the parameters. If this corrects the issue, save the parameters to the keypad using the instructions: <a href=\"#h_01KQSSJ4DFA5YBZ5WS2QQJ0N3C\">Data transfer from the keypad to the replacement VFD<\/a><\/p><h3>Data transfer from the old VFD to the keypad<\/h3><ol>\n<li>Go to parameter 0-50 LCP Copy<\/li>\n<li>Press [OK]<\/li>\n<li>Select [1] All to LCP<\/li>\n<li>Press [OK]<\/li>\n<\/ol><h3>Data transfer from the keypad to the replacement VFD<\/h3><ol>\n<li>Go to parameter 0-50 LCP Copy<\/li>\n<li>Press [OK]<\/li>\n<li>Select [2] All from LCP<\/li>\n<li>Press [OK]<\/li>\n<\/ol><hr><p>*The parameters are also available as a downloadable PDF at the end of the document.<\/p><h2>VFD Parameters<\/h2><h3>Odyssey A2L R454B VFD Parameters<\/h3><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/16010409914509_42526605962125_image.png\" width=\"419\" height=\"769\"><\/figure><h3>Odyssey model 14th digit = B VFD Parameters<\/h3><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/16010409914509_42526605965709_image.png\" width=\"420\" height=\"784\"><\/figure><h3>Odyssey model 14th digit = A VFD Parameters. <\/h3><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/16010409914509_42526644023693_image.png\" width=\"456\" height=\"959\"><\/figure><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. When you apply 24 vac to the G input on the J5 plug, the supply fan will start in low speed. The supply fan will go to full speed by additionally applying 24 vac to the Y2, W1, or W2 terminals. Constant volume operation can be obtained by setting the VFD parameter 4-12 Motor Speed Low Limit to match parameter 4-14 Motor Speed High Limit.<\/p>\n<\/div><\/div><\/section><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical experience and who comply with all federal, state, and local laws, rules, orders, or regulations related to the installation, service, or repair of a heating or central air conditioning product. Voyager III","Voyager","Compressor / Compressor Proving; Wiring / Wire Pairing; Troubleshooting; Sensors / Modules; Communications / BACnet; Installation / Startup; Heating; Manuals / Literature","V3 w/Symbio 700 controls","Large Commercial RTUs | Splits | CSC","2025-09-29","2025-10-16","<p>The Adapter Board used in Symbio 700 Voyager III units has no control logic on it.  It is merely traces that go from Point A to Point B, C, etc.  Its purpose is to make wire harness connections easier.  The Adapter Board distributes power and IMC communications to various modules.  It also provides tie-in points for compressor safeties, pass through for condenser fan contactors, and supplies power for compressor contactors and the VHR relay on MZVAV units.<\/p><p>Below (and attached) is the schematic for the Adapter Board.  Listed below is the function of each connector used in Voyager III units.<\/p><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/39944336108301_39944336106253_image.png\" width=\"869\" height=\"551\"><\/figure><p>J1 - Not used.<\/p><p>J2 - Output to the VHR relay on MZVAV units.  24 VAV power supplied from J8 connector on Adapter Board via a relay on the Symbio 700 (P8)  through J17 on the Adapter board.<\/p><p>J3 - Not used.<\/p><p>J4 - IMC and 24 VAC power for the FOM/IOM.  The 24 VAC is sourced from J15 on the Adapter Board.<\/p><p>J5 - Output to the compressor contactors and feedback from the compressor proving switches from J20 on Adapter Board via Symbio 700 P8, P9, and P10<\/p><p>J6 - Not used<\/p><p>J7 - 24 VAC output for Reheat Pumpout Solenoid on the SMC<\/p><p>J8 - 24 VAC supply input for CC3 coil, CCM, and RHP. from TNS2<\/p><p>J9 - Compressor 3 safeties.<\/p><p>J11 - Compressor 2 safeties<\/p><p>J13 - Compressor 1 safeties<\/p><p>J14 - Not used<\/p><p>J15 - 24 VAC supply input for IOM/FOM from TNS4,<\/p><p>J16 - Condenser Fan Request from Symbio 700<\/p><p>J17 - VHR request from Symbio 700 relay output on P6.<\/p><p>J18 - IMC Communications to the Symbio 700 and 24 VAC in from TNS1.<\/p><p>J19 - 24 VAC Power and IMC to the SMC from J8 on Adapter Board.<\/p><p>J20 - Compressor Request and Contactor Proving from/to Symbio 700 P8, P9, and P10<\/p><p>J21 - Not Used.<\/p><p>J22 - Output to Condenser Fan Contactors.  Fed from J16 on Adapter Board via relays on Symbio 700 P7.  24 VAC fed from J25 on Adapter Board.<\/p><p>J23 - Not used.<\/p><p>J24 - 24 VAC output to Symbio 700 P7 for condenser fan control from J25 on Adapter Board.<\/p><p>J25 - 24 VAC input for CC1 and CC2 contactor coils and condenser fan contactor coils from TNS3.<\/p><p>J26 - Not used<\/p><p>J27 - Not used<\/p><p><span>See attachments for pictorial representations of various functions.<\/span><\/p><figure><img loading=\"lazy\" onerror=\"this.style.display='none'\" src=\"Symbio-700-Library/attachments/39944336108301_40388907577997_image.png\" width=\"855\" height=\"699\"><\/figure><p><strong>WARNING:<\/strong><\/p><p>Information in this article is intended for use by individuals possessing adequate backgrounds of electrical and mechanical   if(t<5.0){for(const p of P){let k=(t-p.st)/.8;if(k<=0)continue;if(k>1)k=1;const e=1-(1-k)*(1-k)*(1-k);cx.fillStyle=p.c;cx.fillRect(p.tx+(1-e)*p.sx,p.ty+(1-e)*p.sy,cs+.6,cs+.6)}}
  else{if(!boom){boom=true;const cX=W/2,cY=Hh/2;for(const p of P){const dx=p.tx-cX,dy=p.ty-cY,d=Math.hypot(dx,dy)||1,sp=(900+Math.random()*2200)*dpr;p.vx=dx/d*sp*(.4+Math.random());p.vy=dy/d*sp*(.4+Math.random())-300*dpr;p.x=p.tx;p.y=p.ty}}
   const q=t-5,al=Math.max(0,1-q/1.3);if(q>.35&&!$('#app').classList.contains('show')){$('#app').classList.add('show');route()}
   if(al<=0){finish();return}cx.globalAlpha=al;for(const p of P){const x=p.x+p.vx*q,y=p.y+p.vy*q+1400*dpr*q*q*.5;cx.fillStyle=p.c;cx.fillRect(x,y,cs+1,cs+1)}cx.globalAlpha=1}
  requestAnimationFrame(f)})(performance.now())};
